\Gamma_{\ell_{{\text{\tiny old}}}}(x,f,M)\subseteq\Gamma_{E\cap\frac{6}{5}B}(x,f,C_{{\text{\tiny old}}}M)\mbox{ for all }B\in\mathcal{W}(B^{\#}),\;x\in(6/5)B.

We apply Lemma
7.12
to the Whitney cover
\mathcal{W}(B^{\#})
of
AB^{\#}
, with
\ell_{0}=\ell_{{\text{\tiny old}}}
and
C_{0}=C_{{\text{\tiny old}}}
. We deduce that

\Gamma_{\ell_{1}}(x,f,M)\subseteq\Gamma_{E\cap AB^{\#}}(x,f,C_{1}M)

for the constants
C_{1}=C\cdot C_{{\text{\tiny old}}}
and
\ell_{1}=\ell_{\text{\tiny old}}+\lceil\frac{\log(D\cdot N+1)}{\log(D+1)}\rceil
, where
N=\#\mathcal{W}(B^{\#})
; here,
C
is a controlled constant.

We prepare to estimate
N=\#\mathcal{W}(B^{\#})
using a volume comparison bound.

For any
B\in\mathcal{W}(B^{\#})
, we have
\operatorname{diam}(B)\geq\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{diam}(B^{\#})
by definition of keystone balls – furthermore, we claim that
\operatorname{diam}(B)\leq 10A\operatorname{diam}(B^{\#})
. We proceed by contradiction: Suppose
\operatorname{diam}(B)>10A\operatorname{diam}(B^{\#})
for some
B\in\mathcal{W}(B^{\#})
. We have
B\cap AB^{\#}\neq\emptyset
by definition of
\mathcal{W}(B^{\#})
. The previous conditions yield that
\frac{6}{5}B\cap B^{\#}\neq\emptyset
. Then
\operatorname{diam}(B)\leq 8\operatorname{diam}(B^{\#})
by the properties of the Whitney cover
\mathcal{W}
(see Definition
2.18
). This completes the proof by contradiction.

For any
B\in\mathcal{W}(B^{\#})
we have
B\cap AB^{\#}\neq\emptyset
and
\operatorname{diam}(B)\leq 10A\operatorname{diam}(B^{\#})
, and therefore
B\subseteq 30AB^{\#}
.

We estimate the volume of
\Omega:=\bigcup_{B\in\mathcal{W}(B^{\#})}\frac{1}{3}B
in two ways. First, note that
\mbox{Vol}(\Omega)\leq\mbox{Vol}(30AB^{\#})=(30A)^{n}\mbox{Vol}(B^{\#})
. Since
\{\frac{1}{3}B\}_{B\in\mathcal{W}}
is pairwise disjoint (by properties of the Whitney cover
\mathcal{W}
),
N=\#\mathcal{W}(B^{\#})
, and
\operatorname{diam}(B)\geq\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{diam}(B^{\#})
for
B\in\mathcal{W}(B^{\#})
, we have

\mbox{Vol}(\Omega)=\sum_{B\in\mathcal{W}(B^{\#})}3^{-n}\mbox{Vol}(B)\geq N6^{-n}\mbox{Vol}(B^{\#}).

Thus,
N\leq(180A)^{n}
. By definition of
\chi
in (
150
),
\ell_{1}=\ell_{\text{\tiny old}}+\lceil\frac{\log(D\cdot N+1)}{\log(D+1)}\rceil\leq\ell_{\text{\tiny old}}+\chi\leq\ell
. Hence,

\Gamma_{\ell}(x,f,M)\subseteq\Gamma_{\ell_{1}}(x,f,M)\subseteq\Gamma_{E\cap AB^{\#}}(x,f,C_{1}M),

as desired.
∎

Lemma 10.15
.

If
\ell\geq\ell_{{\text{\tiny old}}}+\chi
, and if
B^{\#}\in\mathcal{W}
is a keystone ball satisfying
AB^{\#}\subseteq 2B_{0}
, then

\sigma_{\ell}(z_{B^{\#}})\cap V\subseteq CC_{{\text{\tiny old}}}\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\#}},\operatorname{diam}(B^{\#})}.

(154)

Here, the constant
\chi\geq 1
is defined in (
150
), and
C\geq 1
is a controlled constant.

Proof.

Let
C_{0}
be the constant
C
in Lemma
10.14
, and
C^{0}
the constant in Lemma
7.10
. Note that
z_{B^{\#}}\in\frac{6}{5}B^{\#}\subseteq\frac{1}{2}AB^{\#}
(since
A\geq 10
). By condition (
153
) in Lemma
10.14
, and Lemma
7.10
(applied for
B=AB^{\#}
and
x=z_{B^{\#}}
),

\displaystyle\sigma_{\ell}(z_{B^{\#}})

\displaystyle\cap C_{0}C_{{\text{\tiny old}}}\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\#}},A\operatorname{diam}(B^{\#})}

(155)

\displaystyle\subseteq C_{0}C_{{\text{\tiny old}}}(\sigma_{E\cap AB^{\#}}(z_{B^{\#}})\cap\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\#}},A\operatorname{diam}(B^{\#})})

\displaystyle\subseteq C^{0}C_{0}C_{{\text{\tiny old}}}\cdot\sigma(z_{B^{\#}})\qquad\qquad\qquad\mbox{for }\ell\geq\ell_{{\text{\tiny old}}}+\chi.

Apply condition 3 of Lemma
10.2
to
B=B^{\#}
,
x=z_{B^{\#}}
, and
\delta=\operatorname{diam}(B^{\#})
, giving that
\sigma(z_{B^{\#}})
is
(x,\bar{C}\operatorname{diam}(B^{\#}),R_{4})
-transverse to
V
. By Lemma
7.8
,
\sigma(z_{B^{\#}})
is
(x,\operatorname{diam}(B^{\#}),\widehat{R})
-transverse to
V
for
\widehat{R}=\bar{C}^{m}R_{4}
. Therefore,
\sigma(z_{B^{\#}})\cap V\subseteq\widehat{R}\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\#}},\operatorname{diam}(B^{\#})}
. Applying this inclusion and taking the intersection with
V
on each side of (
155
), we obtain

\sigma_{\ell}(z_{B^{\#}})\cap V\cap(C_{0}C_{{\text{\tiny old}}}\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\#}},A\operatorname{diam}(B^{\#})})\subseteq C^{0}C_{0}C_{{\text{\tiny old}}}\widehat{R}\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\#}},\operatorname{diam}(B^{\#})}.

From (
8
),
A\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\#}},\operatorname{diam}(B^{\#})}\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\#}},A\operatorname{diam}(B^{\#})}
(recall
A\geq 1
). Thus,

\sigma_{\ell}(z_{B^{\#}})\cap V\cap(C_{0}C_{{\text{\tiny old}}}A\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\#}},\operatorname{diam}(B^{\#})})\subseteq C^{0}C_{0}C_{{\text{\tiny old}}}\widehat{R}\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\#}},\operatorname{diam}(B^{\#})}.

(156)

By definition of
A
in (
149
),
A=2C^{0}\bar{C}^{m}R_{4}=2C^{0}\widehat{R}
. Therefore, (
156
) reads as

(\sigma_{\ell}(z_{B^{\#}})\cap V)\cap(2C^{0}C_{0}C_{{\text{\tiny old}}}\widehat{R}\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\#}},\operatorname{diam}(B^{\#})})\subseteq C^{0}C_{0}C_{{\text{\tiny old}}}\widehat{R}\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\#}},\operatorname{diam}(B^{\#})}.

Note that
\Omega\cap 2r\mathcal{B}\subseteq r\mathcal{B}\implies\Omega\subseteq r\mathcal{B}
, valid when
\Omega
is a symmetric convex subset of a Hilbert space
X
with unit ball
\mathcal{B}
, and
r>0
. By this fact and the above inclusion, we have

\sigma_{\ell}(z_{B^{\#}})\cap V\subseteq C^{0}C_{0}C_{{\text{\tiny old}}}\widehat{R}\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\#}},\operatorname{diam}(B^{\#})}.

This completes the proof of (
154
) for the controlled constant
C=C^{0}C_{0}\widehat{R}
.

∎

We require one last lemma before the proof of our main result.

Lemma 10.16
.

Let
R,Z\geq 1
and
\lambda\geq 1
be given. If
\Omega
is a symmetric closed convex set in a Hilbert space
X
,
\mathcal{B}
is the closed unit ball of
X
, and
V\subseteq X
is a subspace, satisfying (i)
\mathcal{B}/V\subseteq R\cdot(\Omega\cap\mathcal{B})/V
and (ii)
\Omega\cap V\subseteq Z\mathcal{B}
, then

(\Omega+\lambda\mathcal{B})\cap V\subseteq Z\cdot(3R\lambda+1)\mathcal{B}.

(157)

Proof.

Fix
P\in(\Omega+\lambda\mathcal{B})\cap V
. Write
P=P_{0}+P_{1}
with
P_{0}\in\Omega
and
P_{1}\in\lambda\mathcal{B}
. Since
P_{1}\in\lambda\mathcal{B}
, there exists
P_{2}\in R\lambda(\Omega\cap\mathcal{B})
with
P_{1}-P_{2}\in V
by condition (i). Define
\tilde{P}:=P-(P_{1}-P_{2})\in V
. As
\tilde{P}=P_{0}+P_{2}
, with
P_{0}\in\Omega
and
P_{2}\in R\lambda\cdot\Omega
, we have
\tilde{P}\in(R\lambda+1)\Omega
. Thus, by condition (ii),

\tilde{P}\in(R\lambda+1)\cdot(\Omega\cap V)\subseteq(R\lambda+1)\cdot Z\mathcal{B}.

Therefore,

P=\tilde{P}+P_{1}-P_{2}\in(R\lambda+1)Z\mathcal{B}+\lambda\mathcal{B}+R\lambda\mathcal{B}\subseteq(3R\lambda+1)Z\mathcal{B}.

∎

We finish this section with the proof of Lemma
10.9
.

Proof of Lemma
10.9
.

Fix the constants
A
,
\epsilon_{0}
, and
\chi
as in (
149
), (
150
).

Let
B\in\mathcal{W}_{0}
,
x\in 3B
, and
\ell\geq\ell_{{\text{\tiny old}}}+\chi
. Set
B^{\#}=\kappa(B)\in\mathcal{W}
, as defined in Lemma
10.13
. Thus,
\operatorname{diam}(B^{\#})\leq\operatorname{diam}(B)
,
AB^{\#}\subseteq 2B_{0}
, and
\operatorname{dist}(B^{\#},B)\leq C_{4}\operatorname{diam}(B)
for a controlled constant
C_{4}
. By Lemma
10.15
and (
9
),

\sigma_{\ell}(z_{B^{\#}})\cap V\subseteq CC_{{\text{\tiny old}}}\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\#}},\operatorname{diam}(B^{\#})}\subseteq CC_{{\text{\tiny old}}}\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\#}},\operatorname{diam}(B)}.

(158)

Note that
\operatorname{diam}(B)\leq\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{diam}(B_{0})
(see condition 2 of Lemma
10.2
). We apply condition 3 of Lemma
10.2
, with
B^{\#}\in\mathcal{W}
,
x=z_{B^{\#}}\in\frac{6}{5}B^{\#}
, and
\delta=\operatorname{diam}(B)\in[\operatorname{diam}(B^{\#}),\operatorname{diam}(B_{0})]
. Thus,
\sigma(x)
is
(z_{B^{\#}},\bar{C}\operatorname{diam}(B),R_{4})
-transverse to
V
. By Lemma
7.8
,
\sigma(x)
is
(z_{B^{\#}},\operatorname{diam}(B),\widehat{R})
-transverse to
V
, for
\widehat{R}=\bar{C}^{m}R_{4}
. Hence,

\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\#}},\operatorname{diam}(B)}/V\subseteq\widehat{R}\cdot(\sigma(z_{B^{\#}})\cap\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\#}},\operatorname{diam}(B)})/V.

By the inclusion
\sigma(z_{B^{\#}})\subseteq\sigma_{\ell}(z_{B^{\#}})
, we obtain

\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\#}},\operatorname{diam}(B)}/V\subseteq\widehat{R}\cdot(\sigma_{\ell}(z_{B^{\#}})\cap\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\#}},\operatorname{diam}(B)})/V.

(159)

Since
z_{B^{\#}}\in\frac{6}{5}B^{\#}
and
x\in 3B
, we have

\displaystyle|z_{B^{\#}}-x|

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{dist}(B^{\#},B)+3\operatorname{diam}(B)+(6/5)\operatorname{diam}(B^{\#})

(160)

\displaystyle\leq C_{4}\operatorname{diam}(B)+3\operatorname{diam}(B)+(6/5)\operatorname{diam}(B)

\displaystyle\leq C_{5}\operatorname{diam}(B),

for a controlled constant
C_{5}
.

By Lemma
7.5
and (
160
),
\sigma_{\ell+1}(x)\subseteq\sigma_{\ell}(z_{B^{\#}})+C_{T}\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\#}},C_{5}\operatorname{diam}(B)}
. Then by (
8
),
\sigma_{\ell+1}(x)\subseteq\sigma_{\ell}(z_{B^{\#}})+C_{T}C_{5}^{m}\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\#}},\operatorname{diam}(B)}
. Therefore,

\sigma_{\ell+1}(x)+\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\#}},\operatorname{diam}(B)}\subseteq\sigma_{\ell}(z_{B^{\#}})+\widetilde{C}\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\#}},\operatorname{diam}(B)},

(161)

where
\widetilde{C}=C_{T}C_{5}^{m}+1
is a controlled constant.

We apply Lemma
10.16
to the convex set
\Omega=\sigma_{\ell}(z_{B^{\#}})
in the Hilbert space
X=(\mathcal{P},\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{z_{B^{\#}},\operatorname{diam}(B)})
. We take
\lambda=\widetilde{C}
in Lemma
10.16
. Inclusions (
158
), (
159
) imply hypotheses (i), (ii) of Lemma
10.16
with
R=\widehat{R}
,
Z=CC_{{\text{\tiny old}}}
. So,

\left(\sigma_{\ell}(z_{B^{\#}})+\widetilde{C}\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\#}},\operatorname{diam}(B)}\right)\cap V\subseteq CC_{{\text{\tiny old}}}\cdot(3\widehat{R}\widetilde{C}+1)\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\#}},\operatorname{diam}(B)}.

(162)

From (
161
) and (
162
),

(\sigma_{\ell+1}(x)+\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\#}},\operatorname{diam}(B)})\cap V\subseteq C^{\prime}C_{{\text{\tiny old}}}\cdot\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\#}},\operatorname{diam}(B)},

(163)

for a controlled constant
C^{\prime}
.

Finally, note that
\widehat{C}^{-1}\cdot\mathcal{B}_{z_{B},\operatorname{diam}(B)}\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\#}},\operatorname{diam}(B)}\subseteq\widehat{C}\cdot\mathcal{B}_{z_{B},\operatorname{diam}(B)}
for a controlled constant
\widehat{C}
; these inclusions follow from Lemma
2.16
and the estimate
|z_{B}-z_{B^{\#}}|\leq C\operatorname{diam}(B)
(let
x=z_{B}
in (
160
)). Therefore, (
163
) implies that

(\sigma_{\ell+1}(x)+\mathcal{B}_{z_{B},\operatorname{diam}(B)})\cap V\subseteq CC_{{\text{\tiny old}}}\cdot\mathcal{B}_{z_{B},\operatorname{diam}(B)},

for a controlled constant
C
, as desired. This finishes the proof of Lemma
10.9
.
∎

10.5
Compatibility of the jets
(P_{B})_{B\in\mathcal{W}_{0}}

Our next result states that the polynomials
(P_{B})_{B\in\mathcal{W}_{0}}
are pairwise compatible.

Lemma 10.17
.

There exist constants
\overline{\chi}\geq 5
and
\widetilde{C}\geq 1
, determined by
m
and
n
, such that the following holds. Let
(P_{B})_{B\in\mathcal{W}}
,
\ell^{\#}
, and
\bar{C}_{\ell^{\#}}
be as in the statement of Lemma
10.2
, and suppose
\ell^{\#}\geq\ell_{{\text{\tiny old}}}+\overline{\chi}
. Then
P_{B}-P_{B^{\prime}}\in\widetilde{C}C_{{\text{\tiny old}}}\bar{C}_{\ell^{\#}}M\mathcal{B}_{z_{B},\operatorname{diam}(B)}
for any
B,B^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}_{0}
with
(\frac{6}{5})B\cap(\frac{6}{5})B^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
. Furthermore,
\overline{\chi}=O(\operatorname{poly}(D))
and
\widetilde{C}=O(\exp(\operatorname{poly}(D)))
.

Proof of Lemma
10.17
.

We fix the constants
\epsilon_{0}
and
\chi
via Lemma
10.9
, and let
\overline{\chi}=\chi+5
. Suppose
\ell^{\#}\in\mathbb{N}
is picked so that
\ell^{\#}\geq\ell_{{\text{\tiny old}}}+\overline{\chi}
, and
B,B^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}_{0}
satisfy
\frac{6}{5}B\cap\frac{6}{5}B^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
.

Consider the following two cases for the Whitney cover
\mathcal{W}_{0}\subseteq\mathcal{W}
.

Case 1:

\operatorname{diam}(B)>\epsilon_{0}\operatorname{diam}(B_{0})
for all
B\in\mathcal{W}_{0}
.

Case 2:
There exists
\widehat{B}\in\mathcal{W}_{0}
with
\operatorname{diam}(\widehat{B})\leq\epsilon_{0}\operatorname{diam}(B_{0})
.

Suppose
\mathcal{W}_{0}
is as in Case 1. By the second containment in condition 6 of Lemma
10.2
, we obtain

\displaystyle P_{B}-P_{B^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=(P_{B}-P_{0})+(P_{0}-P_{B^{\prime}})

(164)

\displaystyle\in\bar{C}_{\ell^{\#}}M\mathcal{B}_{z_{B},\operatorname{diam}(B_{0})}+\bar{C}_{\ell^{\#}}M\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\prime}},\operatorname{diam}(B_{0})}.

Because
z_{B},z_{B^{\prime}}\in 2B_{0}
, we have
|z_{B}-z_{B^{\prime}}|\leq 2\operatorname{diam}(B_{0})
. So by Lemma
2.16
, for a controlled constant
C
,

\mathcal{B}_{z_{B^{\prime}},\operatorname{diam}(B_{0})}\subseteq C2^{m-1}\mathcal{B}_{z_{B},\operatorname{diam}(B_{0})}.

(165)

By (
8
), because
\operatorname{diam}(B)>\epsilon_{0}\operatorname{diam}(B_{0})
, we conclude that

\mathcal{B}_{z_{B},\operatorname{diam}(B_{0})}\subseteq(\epsilon_{0})^{-m}\mathcal{B}_{z_{B},\operatorname{diam}(B)}.

(166)

When put together, (
164
), (
165
), (
166
) give that

P_{B}-P_{B^{\prime}}\in\bar{C}_{\ell^{\#}}M(\epsilon_{0})^{-m}C2^{m}\mathcal{B}_{z_{B},\operatorname{diam}(B)}.

Note that
C^{\prime}=(\epsilon_{0})^{-m}C2^{m}
is a controlled constant. We obtain the conclusion of Lemma
10.17
in Case 1, for any choice of
\widetilde{C}\geq C^{\prime}
.

Now suppose
\mathcal{W}_{0}
is as in Case 2. By condition 7 in Lemma
10.2
,

P_{B}-P_{B^{\prime}}=(P_{B}-P_{0})+(P_{0}-P_{B^{\prime}})\in V.

By the first part of condition 6 of Lemma
10.2
,
P_{B^{\prime}}\in\Gamma_{\ell^{\#}-3}(z_{B^{\prime}},f,\bar{C}_{\ell^{\#}}M)
. Because
z_{B}\in\frac{6}{5}B
,
z_{B^{\prime}}\in\frac{6}{5}B^{\prime}
,
\frac{6}{5}B\cap\frac{6}{5}B^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
, and
\operatorname{diam}(B^{\prime})\leq 8\operatorname{diam}(B)
(see condition
(3)
in Definition
2.18
of a Whitney cover) we have

|z_{B}-z_{B^{\prime}}|\leq 16\operatorname{diam}(B).

There exists
\widetilde{P}_{B}\in\Gamma_{\ell^{\#}-4}(z_{B},f,\bar{C}_{\ell^{\#}}M)
with
\widetilde{P}_{B}-P_{B^{\prime}}\in C_{T}\bar{C}_{\ell^{\#}}M\mathcal{B}_{z_{B},16\operatorname{diam}(B)}
, thanks to Lemma
7.5
. By (
8
),
\widetilde{P}_{B}-P_{B^{\prime}}\in 16^{m}C_{T}\bar{C}_{\ell^{\#}}M\mathcal{B}_{z_{B},\operatorname{diam}(B)}
.

By condition 6 in Lemma
10.2
,

P_{B}\in\Gamma_{\ell^{\#}-3}(z_{B},f,\bar{C}_{\ell^{\#}}M)\subseteq\Gamma_{\ell^{\#}-4}(z_{B},f,\bar{C}_{\ell^{\#}}M),

so, because
\widetilde{P}_{B}\in\Gamma_{\ell^{\#}-4}(z_{B},f,\bar{C}_{\ell^{\#}}M)
, by Lemma
7.2
,

\widetilde{P}_{B}-P_{B}\in 2\bar{C}_{\ell^{\#}}M\cdot\sigma_{\ell^{\#}-4}(z_{B}).

Thus,

\displaystyle P_{B}-P_{B^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=(P_{B}-\widetilde{P}_{B})+(\widetilde{P}_{B}-P_{B^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\in 2\bar{C}_{\ell^{\#}}M\cdot\sigma_{\ell^{\#}-4}(z_{B})+16^{m}C_{T}\bar{C}_{\ell^{\#}}M\cdot\mathcal{B}_{z_{B},\operatorname{diam}(B)}

\displaystyle\subseteq C\bar{C}_{\ell^{\#}}M\cdot(\sigma_{\ell^{\#}-4}(z_{B})+\mathcal{B}_{z_{B},\operatorname{diam}(B)}),

and hence

P_{B}-P_{B^{\prime}}\in C\bar{C}_{\ell^{\#}}M\cdot(\sigma_{\ell^{\#}-4}(z_{B})+\mathcal{B}_{z_{B},\operatorname{diam}(B)})\cap V,

for a controlled constant
C
.

Note that
\ell^{\#}-5\geq\ell_{{\text{\tiny old}}}+\overline{\chi}-5=\ell_{{\text{\tiny old}}}+\chi
, by definition of
\overline{\chi}
. We apply Lemma
10.9
(with
\ell=\ell^{\#}-5
) to deduce that

(\sigma_{\ell^{\#}-4}(z_{B})+\mathcal{B}_{z_{B},\operatorname{diam}(B)})\cap V\subseteq CC_{{\text{\tiny old}}}\mathcal{B}_{z_{B},\operatorname{diam}(B)}.

Therefore,
P_{B}-P_{B^{\prime}}\in C^{\prime\prime}C_{{\text{\tiny old}}}\bar{C}_{\ell^{\#}}M\cdot\mathcal{B}_{z_{B},\operatorname{diam}(B)}
for a controlled constant
C^{\prime\prime}
. We obtain the conclusion of Lemma
10.17
in Case 2, for any choice of
\widetilde{C}\geq C^{\prime\prime}
. This concludes the proof of Lemma
10.17
.

∎

10.6
Completing the Main Induction Argument

We complete the induction argument started in Section
10.1
by proving the Main Lemma for
K
. Thus, we fix data
(B_{0},x_{0},E,K,f,\ell^{\#},M,P_{0})
. In Section
10.1
we gave a proof of the Main Lemma for
K
under the assumption
\#(E\cap B_{0})\leq 1
. Thus, we may assume
\#(E\cap B_{0})\geq 2
. See (
139
). Recall our task is to construct a linear map
T:C(E)\times\mathcal{P}\rightarrow C^{m-1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and prove it satisfies (
135
).

The constant
\overline{\chi}
in the Main Lemma for
K
is taken to be
\overline{\chi}
in Lemma
10.17
. Note that
\overline{\chi}\geq 5
is a constant determined by
m
and
n
, and
\overline{\chi}=O(\operatorname{poly}(D))
. Let
\ell^{\#}
,
C^{\#}
satisfy (
136
) for
\overline{\chi}
defined above and
\Lambda
to be defined momentarily.

Given
P_{0}\in\Gamma_{\ell^{\#}}(x_{0},f,M)
, we apply the Main Decomposition Lemma (Lemma
10.2
) to the data
(B_{0},x_{0},E,K,f,\ell^{\#},M,P_{0})
to obtain a Whitney cover
\mathcal{W}
of
2B_{0}
, a DTI subspace
V\subseteq\mathcal{P}
, and families
\{P_{B}\}_{B\in\mathcal{W}}
and
\{z_{B}\}_{B\in\mathcal{W}}
. We defined in (
148
) the subfamily
\mathcal{W}_{0}=\{B\in\mathcal{W}:B\cap B_{0}\neq\emptyset\}
of
\mathcal{W}
, so that
\mathcal{W}_{0}
is a Whitney cover of
B_{0}
.

We apply Lemma
10.3
with
x=z_{B}\in(6/5)B
for
B\in\mathcal{W}
. Thus, there exists a linear map
T_{B}:C(E)\times\mathcal{P}\rightarrow C^{m-1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
satisfying conditions 1,2,3 of Lemma
10.3
, for
x=z_{B}
.

Lemma
10.2
(condition 6) asserts that
P_{B}\in\Gamma_{\ell^{\#}-3}(z_{B},f,\bar{C}_{\ell^{\#}}M)
for
B\in\mathcal{W}
. Because
\ell^{\#}-3\geq\ell^{\#}-\overline{\chi}=\ell_{\text{\tiny old}}
, we have
P_{B}\in\Gamma_{\ell_{\text{\tiny old}}}(z_{B},f,\bar{C}_{\ell^{\#}}M)
. Thus, by Lemma
10.3
, the function
F_{B}:=T_{B}(f,P_{B})\in C^{m-1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
satisfies

\left\{\begin{aligned} &F_{B}=f\mbox{ on }E\cap(6/5)B,\\
&J_{z_{B}}F_{B}=P_{B},\mbox{ and }\|F_{B}\|\leq C_{{\text{\tiny old}}}\bar{C}_{\ell^{\#}}M\end{aligned}\right.\qquad(B\in\mathcal{W}).

(167)

Since
\ell^{\#}\geq\ell_{{\text{\tiny old}}}+\overline{\chi}
, we can apply Lemma
10.17
to conclude that

|J_{z_{B}}F_{B}-J_{z_{B^{\prime}}}F_{B^{\prime}}|_{z_{B},\operatorname{diam}(B)}=|P_{B}-P_{B^{\prime}}|_{z_{B},\operatorname{diam}(B)}\leq\widetilde{C}C_{{\text{\tiny old}}}\bar{C}_{\ell^{\#}}M,

(168)

for
B,B^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}_{0}
with
(6/5)B\cap(6/5)B^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
, and a controlled constant
\widetilde{C}
.

Let
\{\theta_{B}\}_{B\in\mathcal{W}_{0}}
be a partition of unity on
B_{0}
adapted to the Whitney cover
\mathcal{W}_{0}
of
B_{0}
, satisfying the properties in Lemma
2.20
. Define
F:B_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by

F=\sum_{B\in\mathcal{W}_{0}}F_{B}\theta_{B}\mbox{ on }B_{0}.

We describe the basic properties of the function
F
. By Lemma
2.21
and the conditions (
167
), (
168
),
F\in C^{m-1,1}(B_{0})
satisfies
\|F\|_{C^{m-1,1}(B_{0})}\leq CC_{{\text{\tiny old}}}\bar{C}_{\ell^{\#}}M
and
F=f
on
E\cap B_{0}
, where
C
is a controlled constant.

Because each
F_{B}
depends linearly on
(f,P_{B})
, and each
P_{B}
depends linearly on
(f,P_{0})
(see condition 8 in Lemma
10.2
),
F
depends linearly on
(f,P_{0})
.

By conditions 5 and 6 in Lemma
10.2
,
z_{B}=x_{0}
and
P_{B}=P_{0}
if
x_{0}\in(6/5)B
. By the support properties of
\theta_{B}
(see Lemma
2.20
),
J_{x_{0}}\theta_{B}\neq 0\implies x_{0}\in(6/5)B
. Thus,
J_{x_{0}}F_{B}=P_{0}
if
J_{x_{0}}\theta_{B}\neq 0
. Therefore, using that
\sum_{B\in\mathcal{W}_{0}}\theta_{B}=1
on
B_{0}
,

\displaystyle J_{x_{0}}F

\displaystyle=\sum_{B\in\mathcal{W}_{0}:x_{0}\in\frac{6}{5}B}J_{x_{0}}(F_{B}\theta_{B})=\sum_{B\in\mathcal{W}_{0}:x_{0}\in\frac{6}{5}B}J_{x_{0}}F_{B}\odot_{x_{0}}J_{x_{0}}\theta_{B}

\displaystyle=\sum_{B\in\mathcal{W}_{0}:x_{0}\in\frac{6}{5}B}P_{0}\odot_{x_{0}}J_{x_{0}}\theta_{B}=P_{0}\odot_{x_{0}}1=P_{0}.

We extend
F:B_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
to all of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
using Lemma
2.4
(an outcome of the classical Whitney extension theorem). This guarantees the existence of a function
\widehat{F}\in C^{m-1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, depending linearly on
F
, with
\widehat{F}|_{B_{0}}=F
, and

\|\widehat{F}\|_{C^{m-1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C\|F\|_{C^{m-1,1}(B_{0})}\leq C^{\prime}C_{{\text{\tiny old}}}\bar{C}_{\ell^{\#}}M.

Here,
C,C^{\prime}
are controlled constants. By the properties of
F
, stated above, and since
\widehat{F}|_{B_{0}}=F
, we deduce that
\widehat{F}=F=f
on
E\cap B_{0}
and
J_{x_{0}}\widehat{F}=J_{x_{0}}F=P_{0}
(recall
x_{0}\in B_{0}
). Therefore, we have shown:

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\widehat{F}=f\mbox{ on }E\cap B_{0}\\
&J_{x_{0}}\widehat{F}=P_{0}\\
&\|\widehat{F}\|_{C^{m-1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C^{\prime}C_{{\text{\tiny old}}}\bar{C}_{\ell^{\#}}M.\end{aligned}\right.

(169)

We choose
\Lambda
in (
136
), now, to ensure the inequality
C^{\#}\geq C^{\prime}C_{{\text{\tiny old}}}\bar{C}_{\ell^{\#}}
. From Lemma
10.2
recall that
\bar{C}_{\ell^{\#}}=C\cdot(D+1)^{\ell^{\#}}
for a controlled constant
C\geq 1
. From (
136
),
C_{{\text{\tiny old}}}=C^{\#}(K-1)
,
\ell^{\#}=\ell^{\#}(K)
, and
C^{\#}=C^{\#}(K)
have the form
\ell^{\#}=\overline{\chi}\cdot(K+1)
,
C^{\#}=\Lambda^{(K+1)^{2}+1}
and
C_{{\text{\tiny old}}}=\Lambda^{K^{2}+1}
. Thus, the desired inequality is equivalent to

\frac{C^{\#}}{C_{{\text{\tiny old}}}}=\Lambda^{2K+1}\geq C^{\prime}\cdot C\cdot(D+1)^{\overline{\chi}\cdot(K+1)}.

Fix a controlled constant
\Lambda
satisfying the earlier condition (
138
), in addition to
\Lambda\geq C^{\prime}C(D+1)^{\overline{\chi}}
so that the preceding inequality is valid, and
C^{\#}\geq C^{\prime}C_{{\text{\tiny old}}}\bar{C}_{\ell^{\#}}
. Therefore, (
169
) implies

\|\widehat{F}\|_{C^{m-1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C^{\#}M.

Because
\widehat{F}
depends linearly on
F
and
F
depends linearly on
(f,P_{0})
, we have that
\widehat{F}=T(f,P_{0})
for some linear map
T:C(E)\times\mathcal{P}\rightarrow C^{m-1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

Thus we have defined a linear map
T:C(E)\times\mathcal{P}\rightarrow C^{m-1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and verified the conditions in (
135
) (see (
169
)). This completes the proof of the Main Lemma for
K
(Lemma
9.6
).

11
Proofs of the Main Results

11.1
Proof of Theorem
6.1

We give the proof of Theorem
6.1
. Recall that Lemma
9.6
specifies a family of constants
\ell^{\#}(K)
and
C^{\#}(K)
(
K\in\{-1,0,\dots\}
).

Let
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
be finite. Fix a closed ball
B_{0}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
containing
E
, and a point
x_{0}\in B_{0}
. Set
K_{0}:=4mD^{2}
,
\ell^{\#}:=\ell^{\#}(K_{0})
, and
C^{\#}:=C^{\#}(K_{0})
.

By Corollary
9.4
, we have
\mathcal{C}(E|5B_{0})\leq K_{0}
. Lemma
9.6
guarantees the existence of a linear mapping
T:C(E)\times\mathcal{P}\rightarrow C^{m-1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
satisfying, for any
(f,P)\in C(E)\times\mathcal{P}
, if
P\in\Gamma_{\ell^{\#}}(x_{0},f,M)
then

1.

T(f,P)=f
on
E
.

2.

J_{x_{0}}T(f,P)=P
.

3.

\|T(f,P)\|_{C^{m-1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C^{\#}M
.

For the proof of part (A) of Theorem
6.1
, set
k^{\#}:=(D+1)^{\ell^{\#}+3}
. We are given that
f
satisfies the finiteness hypothesis
\mathcal{F}\mathcal{H}(k^{\#},M)
for some
M>0
. According to Lemma
7.5
,
\Gamma_{\ell^{\#}}(x_{0},f,M)\neq\emptyset
. Let
P\in\Gamma_{\ell^{\#}}(x_{0},f,M)
. Set
F=T(f,P)
. According to the above conditions,
F=f
on
E
and
\|F\|_{C^{m-1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C^{\#}M
. Thus,
\|f\|_{C^{m-1,1}(E)}\leq C^{\#}M
. This establishes part (A) of Theorem
6.1
.

We next prove part (B) of Theorem
6.1
. By Lemma
8.3
there exists a linear map
P^{x_{0}}_{\ell^{\#}}:C(E)\rightarrow\mathcal{P}
such that if
f
satisfies
\mathcal{F}\mathcal{H}(k^{\#},M)
then
P^{x_{0}}_{\ell^{\#}}(f)\in\Gamma_{\ell^{\#}}(x_{0},C_{\ell^{\#}}M)
, with
C_{\ell^{\#}}=C^{\prime}(D+1)^{\ell^{\#}}
for a controlled constant
C^{\prime}
.

Define a linear map
\widehat{T}:C(E)\rightarrow C^{m-1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
by
\widehat{T}(f):=T(f,P^{x_{0}}_{\ell^{\#}}(f))
.

Suppose
f\in C(E)
and let
M>\|f\|_{C^{m-1,1}(E)}
. Evidently,
f
satisfies
\mathcal{F}\mathcal{H}(k^{\#},M)
. Hence,
P^{x_{0}}_{\ell^{\#}}(f)\in\Gamma_{\ell^{\#}}(x_{0},C_{\ell^{\#}}M)
. By property 3 of
T
,

\|\widehat{T}(f)\|_{C^{m-1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}=\|T(f,P^{x_{0}}_{\ell^{\#}}(f))\|_{C^{m-1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C^{\#}C_{\ell^{\#}}M=C_{0}^{\#}M,

with
C_{0}^{\#}:=C^{\#}C_{\ell^{\#}}
. Since
M>\|f\|_{C^{m-1,1}(E)}
is arbitrary,
\|\widehat{T}(f)\|_{C^{m-1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C_{0}^{\#}\|f\|_{C^{m-1,1}(E)}
, as desired. By property
1
of
T
, we have
\widehat{T}(f)=f
on
E
. This completes the proof of part (B) of Theorem
6.1
.

We remark at last on the form of the constants. Recall that
C^{\#}=C^{\#}(K_{0})=\Lambda^{(K_{0}+1)^{2}+1}
,
\Lambda
is a controlled constant, and
K_{0}=4mD^{2}
. Thus,
C^{\#}
is a controlled constant. Similarly, since
\ell^{\#}=\ell^{\#}(K_{0})=\overline{\chi}\cdot(K_{0}+1)
with
\overline{\chi}=O(\operatorname{poly}(D))
, we have
\ell^{\#}=O(\operatorname{poly}(D))
, and thus,
C_{\ell^{\#}}=C^{\prime}(D+1)^{\ell^{\#}}
is a controlled constant. Therefore,
k^{\#}=(D+1)^{\ell^{\#}+1}
and
C^{\#}_{0}=C^{\#}C_{\ell^{\#}}
are controlled constants. This completes the proof of Theorem
6.1
.

11.2
Proofs of Theorem
1.3
and
1.4

Let
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
be an arbitrary set, and let
f:E\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
. We claim that

\|f\|_{C^{m-1,1}(E)}=\sup_{\widehat{E}\subseteq E\text{ finite}}\|f|_{\widehat{E}}\|_{C^{m-1,1}(\widehat{E})}.

(170)

To prove (
170
), we use a compactness argument adapted from the proof of Lemma 18.2 of
[
15
]
.

First note that if
\widehat{E}\subseteq E
then
\|f\|_{C^{m-1,1}(E)}\geq\|f|_{\widehat{E}}\|_{C^{m-1,1}(\widehat{E})}
, by definition of the trace seminorm. Therefore, the left-hand side of (
170
) is greater than or equal to the right-hand side of (
170
).

For the reverse inequality, it suffices to demonstrate that

\displaystyle\|f|_{\widehat{E}}\|_{C^{m-1,1}(\widehat{E})}\leq 1\mbox{ for all finite }\widehat{E}\subseteq E

(171)

\displaystyle\implies f\in C^{m-1,1}(E)\mbox{ and }\|f\|_{C^{m-1,1}(E)}\leq 1.

Let
\eta>0
be arbitrary. The hypothesis in (
171
) implies the following:

\displaystyle\mbox{For all finite }\widehat{E}\subseteq E\mbox{ there exists }F_{\widehat{E}}\in C^{m-1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})

(172)

\displaystyle\mbox{satisfying }F_{\widehat{E}}=f\mbox{ on }\widehat{E}\mbox{ and }\|F_{\widehat{E}}\|_{C^{m-1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq 1+\eta.

We define

\mathcal{D}=\{F\in C^{m-1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}):\|F\|_{C^{m-1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq 1+\eta\},

equipped with the local
C^{m-1}
topology defined by the family of seminorms

\rho_{R}(F):=\sup_{|x|\leq R}\max_{|\alpha|\leq m-1}|\partial^{\alpha}F(x)|\qquad(R>0).

We define

\mathcal{D}(x)=\{F\in\mathcal{D}:F(x)=f(x)\}\mbox{ for each }x\in E.

Then (
172
) implies that
\bigcap_{x\in\widehat{E}}\mathcal{D}(x)\neq\emptyset
for any finite subset
\widehat{E}\subseteq E
.

On the other hand, each
\mathcal{D}(x)
is a closed subset of
\mathcal{D}
, and
\mathcal{D}
is compact by the Arzela-Ascoli theorem. Therefore, the intersection of
\mathcal{D}(x)
over all
x\in E
is nonempty. Thus, there exists
F\in C^{m-1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
satisfying
F=f
on
E
and
\|F\|_{C^{m-1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq 1+\eta
. Since
\eta>0
is arbitrary, by definition of the trace seminorm we have
\|f\|_{C^{m-1,1}(E)}\leq 1
.

This completes the proof of (
171
). With this, (
170
) is established.

We take
C^{\#}\geq 1
and
k^{\#}\in\mathbb{N}
as in Theorem
6.1
. Note that the constants
C^{\#}
,
k^{\#}
in Theorem
6.1
satisfy
C^{\#}=O(\exp(\operatorname{poly}(D)))
and
k^{\#}=O(\exp(\operatorname{poly}(D)))
. Thus,
C^{\#},k^{\#}\leq\exp(\gamma D^{k})
for absolute constants
\gamma,k>0
(independent of
m,n,E
).

We first prove Theorem
1.3
. Let
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
be arbitrary, and let
\widehat{E}\subseteq E
be a finite subset. By hypothesis of Theorem
1.3
, we are given
f:E\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
satisfying: For all
S\subseteq\widehat{E}
with
\#(S)\leq k^{\#}
there exists
F^{S}\in C^{m-1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
satisfying
F^{S}=f
on
S
and
\|F^{S}\|_{C^{m-1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq 1
. Then
f|_{\widehat{E}}:\widehat{E}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
satisfies the finiteness hypothesis
\mathcal{F}\mathcal{H}(k^{\#},1)
(see (
102
)). Part (A) of Theorem
6.1
ensures that
\|f|_{\widehat{E}}\|_{C^{m-1,1}(\widehat{E})}\leq C^{\#}
. We deduce that
f\in C^{m-1,1}(E)
and
\|f\|_{C^{m-1,1}(E)}\leq C^{\#}
by (
170
). This completes the proof of Theorem
1.3
.

We will prove Theorem
1.4
for finite
E
. The general case of Theorem
1.4
then follows by a standard argument using Banach limits. See Section 17 of
[
14
]
.

For
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
finite, we write
C(E)
to denote the set of all real-valued functions on
E
. Note that
C(E)=C^{m-1,1}(E)
because
E
is finite. By part (B) of Theorem
6.1
, there exists a linear map
T:C(E)\rightarrow C^{m-1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
satisfying
Tf=f
on
E
and
\|Tf\|_{C^{m-1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C^{\#}\|f\|_{C^{m-1,1}(E)}
for all
f\in C(E)
. This completes the proof of Theorem
1.4
for finite
E
.
